# Thaumaturge Forge 1.20.1 port tracker

## Objective and current status

Port the complete Thaumaturge feature set at the pinned 1.21.1 baseline to Minecraft **1.20.1 on Forge**, preserving gameplay, progression, content, visuals, persistence, and multiplayer behavior. Compilation or reaching the title screen does not constitute completion.

**Implementation is in progress.** Java 17 production and test compilation and full datagen have passed on Forge 47.4.29. The Gradle build runs the original suite, both focused Forge suites, and native GameTests. The current original suite passes all 1,321 cases, including missing cape profile IDs, taint session cleanup, golem press persistence and the Jade golem tooltip. [Cycle 258](#cycle-258-evidence-and-next-work), [Cycle 253](#cycle-253-evidence-and-next-work), [Cycle 224](#cycle-224-evidence-and-next-work), [Cycle 208](#cycle-208-evidence-and-next-work). Packaged multiplayer verifies player state after respawn, dimension transfer and restart, plus jar state after chunk unload and restart. The integrated Ubuntu build passes for the Cycle 216 snapshot. Verification of subsequent fixes and full gameplay acceptance continue. See [cycle 190](#cycle-190-evidence-and-next-work) and [cycle 191](#cycle-191-evidence-and-next-work).

To build the runtime JAR, run `./gradlew runData`, then `./gradlew jarJar` in a separate invocation. The first command populates the ignored generated-resource tree. The second packages it alongside the authored resources.

| Item | Observed value |
| --- | --- |
| Investigation date | 2026-11-17 |
| Repository | `https://github.com/AuramMods/Thaumaturge.git` |
| Working branch | `1.20.1`, created directly from `origin/1.21.1` without tracking that source branch |
| Pinned source commit | `999486b1cff8e34094ed870157657e07adc5f743` (`bump to 1.1.1`) |
| Source platform | Minecraft 1.21.1, NeoForge 21.1.238, Java 21 |
| Source mod version | 1.1.1 |
| Other branch | `main` targets Minecraft 26.1.2 / NeoForge and is farther from the requested target |
| Build | Prism 0.6.4, Gradle wrapper 9.7.0, Spotless 8.9.0 |
| Source inventory | 2,330 main Java files; approximately 254,000 lines |
| Test inventory | 317 test Java files; 1,079 `@Test` and 15 `@ParameterizedTest` annotations (not executed-test counts) |
| Networking / mixins | 42 `playToClient` / `playToServer` registrations in `TTPayloads`; 32 mixin Java files |
| Repository instructions | No repository/ancestor `AGENTS.md`, `docs/MANUAL_CHANGE_PROTOCOL.md`, or root `.codegraph/` found during initial inspection; user-supplied instructions still apply |

The repository ignores `docs`, so the tracked port documents live at the root. Preserve `LICENSE`, `CREDITS.md`, registry identities, and source history. Do not redirect the port to `main` or continuously incorporate moving upstream changes without a concrete reason recorded here.

The official Forge download page listed **47.4.29 latest** and **47.4.28 recommended** for 1.20.1 when inspected. Select and pin an actual tested Forge 47.x release during implementation. Forge's 1.20.1 documentation specifies Java 17; target Java 17 bytecode and runtime compatibility. Build-tool JVM requirements may differ and need a compatible wrapper/plugin combination.

Sources: [Forge downloads](https://files.minecraftforge.net/net/minecraftforge/forge/index_1.20.1.html), [Forge 1.20.1 setup](https://docs.minecraftforge.net/en/1.20.1/gettingstarted/).

Core content must not disappear because a dependency API differs. Port available target integrations and exercise both their presence and absence. Record any genuinely unavailable integration with primary-source evidence, attempted alternatives, and gameplay impact; obtain an explicit user scope decision before treating a feature omission as accepted completion. Do not silently turn optional integrations into required dependencies.

### Sable and Veil on Linux ARM64

Install the pinned Sable Forge build, Create 6.0.8 and Veil 1.0.0.296 slim in the client. On Linux ARM64, also install `imgui-java-natives-linux-arm64-1.86.11.jar` in that client's `mods` folder. Use a Java 17 ARM64 runtime and the matching Linux ARM64 LWJGL natives from the launcher. The ImGui binary links to the system FreeType library, provided by `libfreetype6` on Ubuntu and Debian.

To build the companion JAR on Linux ARM64, install JDK 17, Git, Ant, GNU C/C++ and the FreeType development package. On Ubuntu the system build packages are `git ant g++ libfreetype-dev`. Run `porting/compat/build-imgui-linux-arm64.sh` from the repository root and copy the resulting JAR from `build/compat/`. See [Cycle 292](#cycle-292-evidence-and-next-work) for the tested versions and checksums.

## Work phases

Each phase can require multiple cycles. End **every implementation/fix cycle with its own commit**, including an honest validation summary and a tracker update. Intermediate commits may be incomplete or failing if accurately documented; no empty commits or artificial pass claims.

1. **Baseline and platform:** refine the content/dependency inventory; preserve an immutable baseline; establish Forge/Java 17 build, metadata, datagen, and runnable test configurations. Implement the silent, non-focusing test-launch setup before any graphical game launch.
2. **Foundations:** convert registrations/events, attachments, item data, capabilities, networking, data maps, and shared serialization. Use actual compilation errors and focused behavior tests to drive work.
3. **Gameplay:** port all feature families, recipes, worldgen, dimensions, AI, inventory transactions, equipment, and progression. Maintain coverage against the baseline inventory.
4. **Client and integrations:** finish models, screens, rendering, particles, shaders, mixins, and supported target integrations. Exercise optional dependencies absent and present.
5. **Runtime validation:** automated tests, datagen, headless server tests, silent background client inspection, progression, persistence, multiplayer, and sustained ticking. Fix discovered defects in separate cycles.
6. **Packaged artifact:** install the reobfuscated distributable and declared dependencies into clean Forge 1.20.1 client/server instances; rerun essential scenarios; finish installation/build instructions and evidence. Do not publish a release as part of this goal.

Fresh Forge 1.20.1 worlds and persistence within the port are required. Downgrading an existing Minecraft 1.21.1 world is a separate task and is not assumed safe or part of this goal.

## Silent background game testing

- Use dedicated test profiles/directories and worlds; do not change the user's normal Minecraft profile or system audio. Use `runForgeBackgroundClient` for unattended development sessions. `runClient` is the playable development profile and starts with the window unfocused so it can be selected manually.
- Before **every** graphical launch, provision the test profile with `soundCategory_master:0.0`, `narrator:0`, and `fullscreen:false`. Handle first-run accessibility/narrator setup silently. Keep those settings in effect across restarts. Preserve the mod's normal sound content and functionality.
- Launch without activating the game, launcher, or early loading window. On macOS, a shell background job alone does not prevent window activation. Use a genuinely non-activating launch/window mechanism, including Forge early-window handling and development-only GLFW window hints/hooks if necessary; verify the actual focus behavior. Do not claim `open -g` controls a child Java window without evidence.
- Use background-capable inspection/automation and logs; do not repeatedly foreground the client or inject input into the user's active application. Allow the test world to tick while unfocused where needed.
- Prefer headless dedicated-server/GameTest runs for nonvisual checks, and perform the necessary graphical checks in the background. If a particular visual action truly needs foreground access and no safe alternative works, request a single coordinated session; keep that evidence pending until performed.
- Accept the Minecraft server EULA automatically in dedicated test directories, as explicitly authorized by the user.
- Record commands, process IDs, logs, and outcomes; stop only task-owned game/server processes after use. Keep saves, caches, large logs, and screenshots out of commits; retain useful evidence locally with paths in this tracker.

## Completion requirements

All applicable feature rows must have passing evidence, with no unresolved port-related compile, startup, resource, persistence, progression, rendering, or multiplayer defects. A successful build, a zero-test run, or a title-screen screenshot alone is insufficient. Any accepted exception must be an explicit user decision recorded here, never an assumption made to finish early.

Deliver the tested distributable JAR and checksum, exact Minecraft/Forge/Java/dependency versions, installation and reproducible build/test instructions, completed feature matrix, final commit, and a concise account of verification and any explicitly accepted limitations. Keep the goal incomplete while required behavior or evidence is missing. Do not publish, tag a release, or merge into an unrelated branch merely to finish this goal.

## Cycle 001 evidence and next work

- Build invocation on this host: `env JAVA_HOME=/Library/Java/JavaVirtualMachines/jdk-21.jdk/Contents/Home ./gradlew compileJava processResources --console=plain`. Gradle runs on Java 21; Foojay provisioned Java 17 for compilation. The old daemon criteria forced Java 25 and failed with class version 69; removed those criteria. CI selects Java 17.
- `compile-resolved.log`: configuration, target artifacts, MCP/SRG setup and compiler launch succeed; 62 Java 21 syntax diagnostics in 10 files prevent type checking. This is an incomplete port, not a passing build.

## Cycle 005 evidence and next work

- Added mod-owned `serialization/stream/` codecs rather than introducing fake vanilla/NeoForge classes. Their encoders use the 1.20.1 Netty/FriendlyByteBuf/NBT/registry APIs. Typed composition preserves field order; string/list/map/array decoding enforces bounds, and unknown registry IDs fail explicitly. Codec-backed NBT uses a compound wrapper so scalar values can cross the 1.20.1 network NBT API. This is an internal port protocol, not wire compatibility with NeoForge 1.21.1.
- Migrated 178 source/test files to these codecs, including adapters for vanilla identifiers, keys, UUIDs, positions, directions, item stacks, and ingredients. Particle and item-component stream integration still needs conversion.

Passing coverage includes primitive limits, Minecraft VarInt bytes, malformed/truncated values, Unicode string bounds, optionals, six-field ordering and other composition arities, nested lists, maps and limits, byte arrays, enum IDs, constants, scalar/collection NBT and invalid NBT.

The Gradle wrapper then ran out of its small client heap while deserializing the enormous diagnostic exception; terminated only this task-owned wrapper after recording the actual failure. The subsequent normal `./gradlew compileJava --console=plain` terminates normally with the 2,000-error cap (`main-compile-capped.log`). Future uncapped inventories should run with a larger Gradle client heap.

The server hook wraps its existing post-load processor; the pending block-entity path scopes the native loadStatic call. Native bytecode evidence is in `chunk-loading-bytecode.txt`. This avoids depending on logical-thread guesses for those detached-load paths, but runtime/threaded loading still requires testing.

## Cycle 015 evidence and next work

- Inspected the pinned official 1.21.1 client/server player-interaction bytecode, its item-result enum, the exact NeoForge 21.1.238 userdev patches, and native Forge 47.4.29 server dispatch. The default phase is main-hand-only and depends on the item phase's explicit pass-to-default result. A simple rename of either callback to 1.20.1 `use` would lose that distinction. - Added owned `blockinteraction/BlockInteractions` and `ItemInteractionResult`, preserving all six item-phase outcomes and sided result conversion. The mod registers no custom criteria referring to the newer `DEFAULT_BLOCK_USE` trigger.
- Retained **19 item-phase and 35 default-phase callback bodies**. Six superclass calls now explicitly invoke the owned interface's native-equivalent defaults; the workbench charger explicitly forwards the host's default phase. All other callback source bodies are identical. `interaction-inventory.json` and `callback-body-parity.json` record every callback. Inherited overrides remain dynamically dispatched. Updated the one existing test import to the owned result enum without changing assertions.
- The bridge returns consumed results directly; otherwise it reaches the default callback only for an explicit pass-to-default in the main hand. Non-consuming outcomes return native PASS so Forge can continue to held-item use, matching the observed 1.21 client/server pipeline. Explicit host forwarding preserves the host callback's raw result. This distinction includes FAIL and prevents a candle's skip-default result from removing a held candle.

The second jump on the same drop peaks at height **96.77142** and the server records **20 of 20** on landing.

All five first joins and eight reconnects complete. The small control sample cannot establish the cause. See `build/port-evidence/cycle-258/packaged.log`, `forge-only.log`, both instances' native debug logs and `PortLogin03-first-wait.threads`. The check now treats a missing ID as no UUID match and still recognizes known IDs after display-name changes. The existing development-only name check remains in the same predicate.

## Cycle 260 evidence and next work

- The prior packet traces showed a live TCP login with an outgoing hello and no incoming Forge query. The new probe reads the active Netty selector key and checks the loopback socket queues. Its initial Java agent could not resolve its helper from Netty's loader. That run exited before it could exercise the scheduled ordering. The corrected test agent resolves its helper from the bootstrap loader. It stays outside the distributable.
- `python3 build/port-evidence/cycle-260/packaged-socket-state.py "$PWD/build/port-evidence/cycle-260/scheduled-forge-retry" "$PWD/build/libs/thaumaturge-1.1.1-mc1.20.1-forge.jar" forge 0 --schedule-race` exits **0** against clean Forge 47.4.29 without any mods. The test orders the existing connector and first protocol tasks so the connector stores auto-read false, the protocol task enables it, and the connector then clears read interest. Forge's login task sees auto-read true and leaves the read interest cleared. The client logs an active socket and valid selector key with interest operations zero. The OS shows **131,300 bytes** in the client receive queue. One native `Channel.read()` call lets the same socket complete login. See `build/port-evidence/cycle-260/scheduled-forge-retry.log` and the packet traces under `scheduled-forge-retry/`.
- The client-side mixin queues an explicit read behind Forge's existing login enable for remote TCP connections. It checks the channel's current active and auto-read state on the event-loop thread before reading. The guard limits it to the incoming client connection's login protocol and excludes the integrated server's local channel.
- The same script with `scheduled-baseline`, the Cycle 259 JAR and `packaged 0 --schedule-race` exits **0** after the read probe lets the stalled connection resume. `natural` with the Cycle 259 JAR and arguments `packaged 9` exits **0** without the scheduling agent. It stops at the first captured stall after five successful first joins. The affected sixth socket again has auto-read enabled, selector interest zero and **237,423 bytes** awaiting reads. One explicit read restores login on the same socket. All five warm reconnects pass. The monitors record zero foreground changes over **1,660** samples for the scheduled Forge-only control, **1,839** for the original scheduled package and **14,938** for the natural run.
- `env JAVA_HOME=/Library/Java/JavaVirtualMachines/jdk-21.jdk/Contents/Home ./gradlew -I "$PWD/build/port-evidence/cycle-260/isolated-output.gradle" jarJar runForgePortTests runForgePortClientTests -PportHiddenWindow --console=plain` exits **0**. The init script directs the package to a separate directory while the old package remains in use. All **628** focused server tests and **729** focused client tests pass. The reobfuscated JAR's refmap resolves the new injection to Forge's target method. Its SHA-256 is `f470a649e5590e653f038d244d9cede25b4b9d64285de35604b9c6423798f694`. The tested bytes are also at the standard `build/libs/thaumaturge-1.1.1-mc1.20.1-forge.jar`. See `build-and-network-tests.log`, `focused-server/` and `focused-client/`.
- `python3 build/port-evidence/cycle-260/packaged-socket-state.py "$PWD/build/port-evidence/cycle-260/scheduled-fixed" "$PWD/build/port-evidence/cycle-260/fixed-package/thaumaturge-1.1.1-mc1.20.1-forge.jar" packaged 1 --schedule-race --expect-first` exits **0**. One joins while the other stays online, then reconnects through the native disconnect screen. The hidden, muted profiles have narration and fullscreen off. - Forge posts `OnDatapackSyncEvent` before submitting the vanilla tag packet on both join and reload. The pinned Sable Forge build sends its tag-dependent physics definitions from that event. The Cycle 286 original replay receives the bundled values on join, then reproduces stale properties on reload. ## Cycle 289 evidence and next work

Veil registers a blit program that fails the actual OpenGL link-status query on startup and after native client resource reload. The bundled program definition supplies the vertex ID in `location`. Veil's Forge 1.20.1 deserializer reads `path` and discards the missing vertex stage. The linker reports that the fragment stage never receives `texCoord`. The optional client mixin supplies the bundled vertex path through Veil's existing deserializer and keeps its GLSL compilation path. [Veil's pinned sources and assets](https://maven.blamejared.com/foundry/veil/Veil-forge-1.20.1/1.0.0.296/Veil-forge-1.20.1-1.0.0.296-sources.jar)
- On this client OpenGL reports **4.1 Metal - 91.7** and Veil reports compute support as false. Veil's point-light registry creates its native `InstancedPointLightRenderer` both at startup and after resource reload. The separate compute program is still registered after its attempted compile fails. Apple documents OpenGL 4.1 as the limit on Apple Silicon. [Apple Silicon graphics support](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2020/10631/?time=1465)

## Cycle 290 evidence and next work

- Veil 1.0.0.296's shader manager registers a program even when compilation fails, as reproduced on the packaged macOS client in Cycle 289. The optional mixin checks Veil's actual render-thread compute capability before creating or recompiling its built-in indirect sphere culling program. It frees any previous instance at that ID when the newly selected definition is an unsupported compute program. A graphics definition at the same ID continues through Veil's existing compiler. Thaumaturge uses the named Veil JAR already produced by the pinned Sable build as a compile-only dependency. [Veil's pinned sources](https://maven.blamejared.com/foundry/veil/Veil-forge-1.20.1/1.0.0.296/Veil-forge-1.20.1-1.0.0.296-sources.jar)

Its shared Veil vertex declares an explicit location for `texCoord` while built-in GLSL fragments consume the varying by name. Thaumaturge now normalizes that vertex declaration for GLSL compilation. ## Cycle 292 evidence and next work

- Cycle 290's packaged ARM64 client crashes during Veil's resource editor registration. Veil 1.0.0.296 overrides ImGui's platform-dependent filename with `libimgui-javaarm64.dylib` for every ARM JVM and Linux rejects the Mach-O library with an invalid ELF header. The same registration loads the binding when the ImGui overlay is disabled. Thaumaturge restricts Veil's filename override to macOS and respects an explicitly configured name. [Veil 1.0.0.296 source distribution](https://maven.blamejared.com/foundry/veil/Veil-forge-1.20.1/1.0.0.296/Veil-forge-1.20.1-1.0.0.296-sources.jar), [ImGui Java 1.86.11 library resolution](https://github.com/SpaiR/imgui-java/blob/v1.86.11/imgui-binding/src/main/java/imgui/ImGui.java)
- The Linux ARM companion build fetches ImGui Java 1.86.11 at `2cef96d9936fdfcbedb3f9327d2463c6bf6f280e` and its pinned submodules. The patch selects gdx-jnigen's ARM64 GNU target and retains the Java 8 binding bytecode target when building with JDK 17. The game library contains the matching JNI binary and upstream notices. The first build container was missing Git, which the upstream build uses for its version. The corrected container includes Git and compiles the ELF library with FreeType enabled. [Pinned native build generator](https://github.com/SpaiR/imgui-java/blob/2cef96d9936fdfcbedb3f9327d2463c6bf6f280e/buildSrc/src/main/groovy/tool/generator/GenerateLibs.groovy)

## Cycle 294 evidence and next work

- The packaged macOS editor was inactive while the test machine's displays were asleep. GLFW allows its monitor query to return null. ImGui Java 1.86.11 clears its current monitor list and then dereferences that result. The failure occurs during Veil's renderer construction, after the native library has loaded. The optional Thaumaturge mixin treats a null buffer as zero entries so the backend completes its normal update and installs the monitor callback. The original iteration still reads GLFW's monitor geometry and scaling when the buffer contains monitors. The worker remains within **0.2 blocks** horizontally and **0.1 blocks** vertically of an unadvanced waypoint for up to **16 consecutive ticks**, then loses deck contact and retains the full seven-ingot load. The probe records each tick after native entity travel in `server/navigation.tsv`. Its first implementation called the package-private frame class directly, and the corrected probe uses the published frame interface.
- Sable projects exact steering positions into the world and also has a block-position projection that rounds to the world grid. The ordinary arrival test uses that rounded position. Sable advances the ship's physics before entity travel, and travel later applies the ship's movement to each living entity. An intermediate build using the current pose for arrival reproduces the stall at the first waypoint. A further steering experiment using the previous pose produces jumps and loses the native worker lookup. Those attempts are in the `fixed-tick*` and `pose-tick*` profiles. [Pinned Sable waypoint projection](https://github.com/NotAsher999/sable-1.20.1/blob/e4d2ab3724a9f5820e758646447d3a571761db11/common/src/main/java/dev/ryanhcode/sable/mixin/entity/entity_pathfinding/PathMixin.java), [Pinned ship physics tick](https://github.com/NotAsher999/sable-1.20.1/blob/e4d2ab3724a9f5820e758646447d3a571761db11/common/src/main/java/dev/ryanhcode/sable/sublevel/system/SubLevelPhysicsSystem.java), [Pinned living entity travel hook](https://github.com/NotAsher999/sable-1.20.1/blob/e4d2ab3724a9f5820e758646447d3a571761db11/common/src/main/java/dev/ryanhcode/sable/mixin/entity/entity_sublevel_collision/LivingEntityMixin.java)
- The `arrival-tick*` builds keep Sable's steering and use the previous deck pose for waypoint arrival. All three moving profiles still time out. The trace reveals a path origin at plot Y **130** with the worker standing at plot Y **129**. Sable's walk evaluator was projecting the worker into the ship's new pose before the worker traveled with it. The matching stationary ship and ordinary-world profiles deliver both batches across restart. [Pinned Sable walk evaluator](https://github.com/NotAsher999/sable-1.20.1/blob/e4d2ab3724a9f5820e758646447d3a571761db11/common/src/main/java/dev/ryanhcode/sable/mixin/entity/entity_pathfinding/WalkNodeEvaluatorMixin.java)
- The optional Sable mixins now use the previous deck pose to choose a walkable starting block for grounded golems and to check arrival at waypoints on their current deck. The start uses vanilla's walkability check and node initialization. Sable still steers the golem, and vanilla still checks for stuck navigation.
